and (RE2). For instance, if
K
and each
r_{k}
for
k\in[K]
are constants with
d_{1}\asymp\cdots\asymp d_{K}\asymp T
and all factors are pervasive (i.e.,
\alpha_{k,j}=1
for each
j\in[r_{k}]
and
k\in[K]
), then the extra rate assumptions in Theorem
9
become

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[d_{\text{-}k}]}\big\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon,j}^{(k)}\big\|+S_{\psi}^{(k)}=o(g_{s}),\;\;\;r_{e}S_{\psi}^{(k)}=o(Tg_{s}),

which are in fact far more relaxed than (RE2) and those rate assumptions in Theorem
6
.

5.2
Practical implementation for core rank estimator

Since there is only one mode-
k
unfolding matrix from our data, we propose a method for Bootstrapping the mode-
k
fibres given our current data, so that we can search for
\eta_{T}
using the Bootstrapped data. Consider for
b=1,\ldots,B
(
B>0
is a pre-chosen integer), a sequence of independent and identically distributed Bernoulli random variables
\{\xi_{j}^{(b)}\}_{j=1,\ldots,d_{\text{-}k}}
, with
\{\xi_{j}^{(b_{1})}\}
independent of
\{\xi_{j}^{(b_{2})}\}
for
b_{1}\neq b_{2}
. We then create a random matrix
\mathbf{W}_{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{-}k}\times d_{\text{-}k}}
, where the
i
-th column is
\mathbf{0}
except its
j
-th zero (with
j
chosen uniformly from
[d_{\text{-}k}]
)
is replaced by
\xi_{i}^{(b)}
.
We then create the new projected data

\mathbf{y}_{t,m+1,b}^{(k)}:=\text{mat}_{k}(\mathcal{X}_{t}-\bar{\mathcal{X}})\mathbf{W}_{b}\mathbf{W}_{b}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\check{\mathbf{q}}_{\text{-}k}^{(m)}.

Essentially, we Bootstrap the mode-
k
fibres by choosing them randomly with replacement, and augment the vector of projection
\check{\mathbf{q}}_{\text{-}k}^{(m)}
accordingly by pre-multiplying it with
\mathbf{W}_{b}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
.

From here on, we drop the subscript
m+1
for the ease of presentation. We then create, for each
k\in[K]
and
b\in[B]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y,b}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\mathbf{y}_{t,b}^{(k)}\Big)^{\otimes 2},\;\;\;\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)}:=\text{diag}^{-1/2}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y,b}^{(k)})\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y,b}^{(k)}\text{diag}^{-1/2}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y,b}^{(k)}).

For a constant
C
, we then calculate

\displaystyle\widehat{r}_{k}^{(b)}(C):=\max\{j:\lambda_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})>1+CT^{-1/2},\;j\in[d_{k}]\}.

We propose to choose
C
with

\widehat{C}:=\min_{C>0}\widehat{\text{var}}(\{\widehat{r}_{k}^{(b)}(C)\}_{b\in[B]}),

where
\widehat{\text{var}}(\{x_{t}\}_{t\in\mathcal{T}})
is the sample variance of
\{x_{t}\}_{t\in\mathcal{T}}
. Finally, our estimator for
r_{k}
is defined to be

\check{r}_{k}:=\text{Mode of }\{\widehat{r}_{k}^{(b)}(\widehat{C})\}_{b\in[B]}.

(5.6)

The intuition of
\widehat{C}
and
\check{r}_{k}
is as follows. If there are
r_{k}
factors for
\mathbf{A}_{k}
, then the first
r_{k}
eigenvalues of
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)}
for each
b\in[B]
should be approximately well-separated. Setting a large
C
will create a large threshold
1+CT^{-1/2}
that is almost always lying in between
\lambda_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})
and
\lambda_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})
for some fixed
j\in[r_{k}]
for each
b\in[B]
, so that
\widehat{\text{var}}(\{\widehat{r}_{k}^{(b)}(C)\}_{b\in[B]})
will be small, or even equals 0.

However, if
C
is small such that
1+CT^{-1/2}
is now in between
\lambda_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})
and
\lambda_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})
for some
j\in[d_{k}]/[r_{k}]
and some
b\in[B]
, then we expect that this particular threshold will lie in between
\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})
and
\lambda_{j^{\prime}+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})
for some
j^{\prime}\neq j
and some others
b\in[B]
, since all these eigenvalues are less than or equal to 1 by Theorem
8
, and their variability is originated from the noise series only, making them less stable compared to when
j\in[r_{k}]
. Hence for a small enough
C
, we expect
\widehat{\text{var}}(\{\widehat{r}_{k}^{(b)}(C)\}_{b\in[B]})
to be large. The range of values of
C
such that
1+CT^{-1/2}
lies in between
\lambda_{r_{k}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})
and
\lambda_{r_{k}+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{y,b}^{(k)})
for the majority of
b\in[B]
will then include
\widehat{C}
. The definition of
\check{r}_{k}
in (
5.6
) allows for variability arises from the noises and the
r_{k}
-th factor which can be weak and hence may not be detected in all Bootstrap samples.

Finally, in all the simulation settings in Section
6
,we use
B=50
Bootstrap samples. This is a safe number, since reducing it to 10 in fact hardly change the results in our simulation experiments.

6
Simulation Experiments

In this section,
we conduct simulation experiments to compare the performances of our pre-averaging estimators (PRE) and iterative projection estimators (PROJ) to other state-of-the-art competitors. We also test the performance of our proposed rank estimators (BCorTH) with bootstrapping of tensor fibres for tuning parameter selection. A real data analysis is also carried out in Section
6.4
.

6.1
Simulation settings

For generating our data, we use model (
3.1
), with elements in
\mu
being i.i.d. standard normal in each repetition of experiment. For
k\in[K]
, each factor loading matrix
\mathbf{A}_{k}
is generated independently with
\mathbf{A}_{k}=\mathbf{B}_{k}\mathbf{R}_{k}
, where the elements in
\mathbf{B}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times r_{k}}
are i.i.d.
U(u_{1},u_{2})
, and
\mathbf{R}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times r_{k}}
is diagonal with the
j
th diagonal element being
d_{k}^{-\zeta_{k,j}}
,
0\leq\zeta_{k,j}\leq 0.5
. Pervasive (strong) factors have
\zeta_{k,j}=0
, while weak factors have
0<\zeta_{k,j}\leq 0.5
.

The elements in
\mathcal{F}_{t}
are independent standardized AR(5) with AR coefficients 0.7,0.3,-0.4,0.2 and -0.1, and the corresponding innovation process is i.i.d. standard normal. Same for the elements in
\mathbf{e}_{t}^{(k)}
and
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{t,\ell}^{(k)}
in (
3.5
), but their AR coefficients are (-0.7,-0.3,-0.4,0.2,0.1) and (0.8,0.4, -0.4,0.2,-0.1) respectively. The matrix
\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(1)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r_{e}}
is generated with i.i.d. standard normal entries, but has an independent probability of 0.7 being set exactly to 0. Each matrix
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon,\ell}^{(1)}
has randomly generated eigenvectors with eigenvalues on
U(1,3)
. Each experiment is repeated 500 times.

We consider the simulation setting (I), (II) and (III), with sub-setting (a) and (b), detailed below:

(Ia)

All strong factors with
\zeta_{k,j}=0
for all
k,j
.
u_{1}=-2
,
u_{2}=2
so that columns of
\mathbf{A}_{k}
sum to normal magnitude (small
s_{k}
).

(IIa)

One strong factor with
\zeta_{k,1}=0
and
\zeta_{k,2}=0.2
for all
k
.
u_{1}=-2
,
u_{2}=2
.

(IIIa)

Two weak factors with
\zeta_{k,1}=0.1
and
\zeta_{k,2}=0.2
for all
k
.
u_{1}=-2
,
u_{2}=2
.

(Ib)

Same as (Ia), except that
u_{1}=0
,
u_{2}=2
so that column sums of
\mathbf{A}_{k}
have large magnitude (large
s_{k}
).

(IIb)

Same as (IIa), except that
u_{1}=0
,
u_{2}=2
.

(IIIb)

Same as (IIIa), except that
u_{1}=0
,
u_{2}=2
.

For all profiles above, we set
r_{k}=2
for all
k
, and let
r_{e}=10
. Each profile will be considered for the following four settings of different dimensions:

i.

K=2
,
T=100
,
d_{1}=d_{2}=40
;

ii.

K=2
,
T=200
,
d_{1}=d_{2}=40
;

iii.

K=2
,
T=200
,
d_{1}=d_{2}=80
;

iv.

K=3
.
T=200
,
d_{1}=d_{2}=d_{3}=20
.

6.2
Results for settings with
K=2

For our pre-averaging estimation procedure, we set
M_{0}=200
,
M=5
and
n_{l}=\frac{d_{l}}{2}
(refer to Section
3.3.2
and Remark
3
for the meaning of these parameters. See also Section
4.2
). We have tried to increase
M_{0}
and
M
10 folds in all our settings, but there are no distinguishable improvements. We first calculate the pre-averaging estimator
\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{k,pre}
for each
k\in[K]
, and obtain
\check{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{(0)}=\widehat{\mathbf{q}}_{k,pre}=\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{k,pre,(1)}
. Then calculate
\check{\mathbf{q}}_{k}^{(m)}
according to the Algorithm for Iterative Projection Direction Refinement in Section
4.1
for
m=1,\ldots,29
. Finally, we obtain the iterative projection estimator
\check{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
by utilising
\check{\mathbf{q}}_{\text{-}k}^{(29)}
as the projection direction for each
k\in[K]
. We compare the pre-averaging estimators
\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{k,pre}
and the iterative projection estimator
\check{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
with the TOPUP/TIPUP estimators in
Chen et al., 2022
and the iTOPUP/iTIPUP iterative estimators in
Han et al., 2020
. We also compare to the Higher Order Singular Value Decomposition (HOSVD) and the Higher Order Orthogonal Iterations (HOOI) (see
Shen et al., 2016
for example), although these two methods still lack theoretical guarantees for time series tensor factor models. For estimation accuracy, we use the measure
\|\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{k}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}-\mathbf{U}_{k}\mathbf{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\|
, i.e. the spectral norm of the difference of the estimated and true underlying projection matrices, where
\mathbf{U}_{k}
is obtained using the singular value decomposition of
\mathbf{A}_{k}
.

Figure 1:
Plot of estimation error
\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}-\mathbf{U}_{2}\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\|
(in log-scale) for Setting (I).
Left:
Sub-setting (a).
Right:
Sub-setting (b).

We omit the results for
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and only display the estimation accuracy for
\mathbf{A}_{2}
in Figures
1
to
3
since the results for
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
are very similar. We also omit comparing to TOPUP and iTOPUP in the end since their performances are far worse than TIPUP and iTIPUP in all settings.

From Figure
1
, when all factors are strong (pervasive), all estimators perform well, but HOOI outperforms all methods in sub-setting (a) when the elements of
\mathbf{A}_{2}
are i.i.d.
U(-2,2)
, with our iterative projection estimator and iTIPUP follow closely. All methods improve in absolute terms also when
T
or
d_{1},d_{2}
increase. The relative advantage of HOOI over the iterative projection estimator is largely removed under sub-setting (b) where the elements of
\mathbf{A}_{2}
are i.i.d.
U(0,2)
. The main difference between sub-setting (a) and (b) is that the mean of each element of sub-setting (a) is 0 while it is non-zero in sub-setting (b). Whenever the mean of the elements are not 0, our methods, including the pre-averaging estimator, can take advantage since our methods are based in summing rows of
\mathbf{A}_{1}
(for estimating
\mathbf{A}_{2}
) or
\mathbf{A}_{2}
(for estimating
\mathbf{A}_{1}
).

Figure 2:
Plot of estimation error
\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}-\mathbf{U}_{2}\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\|
(in log-scale) for Setting (II).
Left:
Sub-setting (a).
Right:
Sub-setting (b).

The picture changes substantially when one of the pervasive factors are made weak factor for
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
in setting (II) (Figure
2
). In sub-setting (a), our iterative projection method is now as good as HOOI, which are both better than iTIPUP. In sub-setting (b), with our methods taking advantage of the non-zero mean of the elements in
\mathbf{A}_{1}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}
, the pre-averaging estimator and the iterative projection estimator are both performing better than the HOOI for
T=100,200
and
d_{k}=40
, while our iterative projection estimator is still on par with HOOI when
d_{k}=80
. TIPUP and iTIPUP are now performing far worse compared to our estimators and HOOI.

Figure 3:
Plot of estimation error
\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}-\mathbf{U}_{2}\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\|
(in log-scale) for Setting (III).
Left:
Sub-setting (a).
Right:
Sub-setting (b).

The picture changes drastically again under setting (III) (Figure
3
), when all factors are weak. Both our iterative projection estimator and iTIPUP are now performing worse than HOOI, with large variances too, under sub-setting (a). Except for HOOI, all methods also fail to get better as
T
or
d_{k}
increase, showing the difficulty of the setting. However, under sub-setting (b), our iterative projection method now works the best and show descent performances under different
T
and
d_{k}
, while all other methods are failing.

Mean and SD (in bracket, presented as actual values multiplied by 100) of the run time (in seconds) for factor loading estimations under different methods and different dimensions with

.

Next we compare computational time for different methods. Table
1
shows the average time needed to compute the three different initial estimators, namely, our pre-averaging estimator (PRE), the TIPUP estimator, and the HOSVD. It also shows the average time needed to compute the iterative estimators corresponding to these initial estimators, namely, our iterative projection estimator (PROJ), the iTIPUP, and the HOOI. Under our different settings with
K=2
, we see that our pre-averaging estimator takes up the most computational time, while the iterative step is fast. The opposite is true for TIPUP/iTIPUP and HOSVD/HOOI, where their iterative steps are slow but take less time to calculate an initial estimator.

Finally, we compare the accuracy of our bootstrapped rank estimator in (
5.6
) to the iTIP-ER in
Han et al., 2022
. We abbreviate our method as BCorTh, which stands for Bootstrapped Correlation Thresholding. The projection vector used for constructing
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{y,m+1}^{(k)}
is obtained from our iterative projection estimator as before, and the bootstrap is conducted 50 times. We run the simulation for 500 times for each setting, and report the mean and standard deviation of
\widehat{r}_{1}
,
\widehat{r}_{2}
, and the computational time for BCorTh and iTIP-ER. Table
2
shows the results. We can see that BCorTh generally outperforms iTIP-ER in most settings in terms of correct proportion. The only exception is Setting (IIIa), where iTIP-ER is better especially with large
T
and
d_{k}
.
In general, iTIP-ER performs very poorly in Setting (IIb) and (IIIb), while BCorTh performs relatively poorly in Setting (IIIa).

Regarding the effect of
T
and
d_{k}
, in Setting (I) and (II), both BCorTh and iTIP-ER give better performance when
T
and
d_{k}
increase. Setting (III) is different: In Setting (IIIa), increasing
T
and
d_{k}
largely enhances the performance of iTIP-ER, but not for BCorTh. In Setting (IIIb), increasing
T
and
d_{k}
largely enhances the performance of BCorTh, while it negatively affects the performance of iTIP-ER.

Also, in all settings except (IIIa), BCorTh and iTIP-ER generally tend to under-estimate the true number of factors. In Setting (IIIa), iTIP-ER tends to over-estimate
r_{k}
, while BCorTh also over-estimates
r_{k}
when
d_{k}=80
, but under-estimates
r_{k}
with smaller
d_{k}
and
T
.

6.4
Real data analysis

We analyze a set of Fama-French portfolio returns data formed on size and operating profitability. Stocks are categorized into 10 different sizes (market equity, using NYSE market equity deciles) and 10 different operating profitability (OP) levels (using NYSE OP deciles. OP is annual revenues minus cost of goods sold, interest expense, and selling, general, and administrative expenses divided by book equity for the last fiscal year end). These levels, and hence the stocks in each category, are allocated at the end of June each year. Moreover, the stocks in each of the
10\times 10
categories form exactly two portfolios, one being value weighted, and the other of equal weight.
Hence, there are two sets of
10\times 10
portfolios with their time series of returns observed. We use monthly data from July 1973 to June 2021, so that
T=576
, and each data tensor we have thus has size
10\times 10\times 576
. For more details, please visit

https://mba.tuck.dartmouth.edu/pages/faculty/ken.french/Data_Library/det_100_port_szme_op.html

Since the market factor is certainly pervasive in financial returns, we use the following CAPM to remove its effects and facilitate detection of potentially weaker factors:

\mathbf{y}_{t}=\bar{\mathbf{y}}+\boldsymbol{\beta}(x_{t}-\bar{x})+\mathbf{e}_{t},

where
\mathbf{y}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{100}
contains the returns of the 100 portfolios at time
t
,
x_{t}
is the return for the NYSE composite index at time
t
, and
\boldsymbol{\beta}=(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{100})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
is the vector of
\beta
’s for the 100 portfolios. Least squares estimation leads us to

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}(x_{t}-\bar{x})(\mathbf{y}_{t}-\bar{\mathbf{y}})}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}(x_{t}-\bar{x})^{2}}.

Hence the data we analyze is
\{\mathbf{y}_{t}-\bar{\mathbf{y}}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(x_{t}-\bar{x})\}_{t=1,\ldots,576}
, with each observed vector reshaped into a
10\times 10
tensor.

, we show the estimated loading matrices after a varimax rotation that maximizes the variance of the squared factor loadings, scaled by 30 for a cleaner view. To save space, we only show the results for value weighted portfolios in Table

Table 7:

From Table
6
, we can see that from PROJ and HOOI, OP1 itself (possibly OP2 as well) forms one group while OP6 to OP10 form another. iTIPUP also gives a clear grouping effect but is a bit different from the other two methods. Nevertheless, we can say that OP1 and OP2 possibly represents “low operating profitability”, while OP6 to OP10 are “high operating profitability”, and are governed by different factors. For Size, from the three methods, S1 to S5 form one group (“small size”) while another group contains at least S9 and S10 (“large size”), possibly S6 to S8 as well.

7
Appendix: Basic Tensor Manipulations

We briefly introduce the notations and review on tensor manipulations in this section just enough for the presentation of this paper. For more information, please refer to
Kolda and Bader, 2009
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times\cdots\times d_{K}}
be an order-
K
tensor. Here
K
represents the number of dimensions in
\mathcal{X}_{t}
, also called the number of
modes
. For instance, a vector time series has
K=1
while a matrix time series has
K=2
. If we write
\mathcal{X}=(x_{i_{1}\cdots i_{K}})
, then we define a
mode-
k
fibre
of
\mathcal{X}
to be a column vector (of length
d_{k}
)

\displaystyle(x_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k-1},j,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{K}})_{j\in[d_{k}]},\;\;\;i_{\ell}\in[d_{\ell}]\;\text{ with }\ell\in[K],

where we define, for any positive integer
n
,
[n]:=\{1,\ldots,n\}
. Hence there are in total
d_{\text{-}k}:=\prod_{\ell=1\;;\ell\neq k}^{K}d_{\ell}
number of mode-
k
fibres for the tensor
\mathcal{X}
. The
mode-
k
unfolding matrix

\mathbf{X}_{(k)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times d_{\text{-}k}}
(also denoted as
\text{mat}_{k}(\mathcal{X})
in this paper) is then defined to be the matrix containing (in order) all the mode-
k
fibres of
\mathcal{X}
.

If there is a matrix
\mathbf{A}=(a_{ij})\in\mathbb{R}^{I_{k}\times r_{k}}
,
k\in[K]
, and
\mathcal{F}=(f_{i_{1}\cdots i_{K}})\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots r_{K}}
is an order-
K
tensor,
then the
k
-mode product
of
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathbf{A}
, denoted by
\mathcal{F}\times_{k}\mathbf{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{k-1}\times I_{k}\times r_{k+1}\times\cdots\times r_{K}}
, is defined as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}\times_{k}\mathbf{A})_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k-1},j,i_{k+1}\cdots i_{K}}:=\sum_{i_{k}=1}^{r_{k}}f_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}\cdots i_{K}}a_{ji_{k}}.

As an example, consider
K=2
, so that
\mathcal{F}
is a matrix. Let
\mathbf{A}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{I_{1}\times r_{1}}
and
\mathbf{A}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{I_{2}\times r_{2}}
. Then the mode-1 fibres of
\mathcal{F}
are in fact the columns of
\mathcal{F}
, while the mode-2 fibres of
\mathcal{F}
are the rows of
\mathcal{F}
(made column vectors). We also have

\mathcal{F}\times_{1}\mathbf{A}_{1}=\mathbf{A}_{1}\mathcal{F},\;\;\;\mathcal{F}\times_{2}\mathbf{A}_{2}=\mathcal{F}\mathbf{A}_{2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}=(\mathbf{A}_{2}\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}.

Note that the two unfolded matrices (mode-1 and mode-2 respectively) are
\mathbf{F}_{(1)}=\mathcal{F}
and
\mathbf{F}_{(2)}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
, hence the above also means that

\mathcal{F}\times_{1}\mathbf{A}_{1}=\mathbf{A}_{1}\mathbf{F}_{(1)},\;\;\;\mathcal{F}\times_{2}\mathbf{A}_{2}=(\mathbf{A}_{2}\mathbf{F}_{(2)})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}.

In general, to calculate
\mathcal{F}\times_{k}\mathbf{A}
, we find the mode-
k
unfolding matrix
\mathbf{F}_{(k)}
first and then calculate
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{F}_{(k)}
, which contains all mode-
k
fibres of
\mathcal{F}
being pre-multiplied by
\mathbf{A}
. Then we put the columns in
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{F}_{(k)}
back into the original shape of the tensor
\mathcal{F}
. Hence for
K=2
, to put the columns in
\mathbf{A}\mathbf{F}_{(2)}
back into the original orientation of the tensor (rows), we take transpose of it, so that
\mathcal{F}\times_{2}\mathbf{A}=(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{F}_{(2)})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
.
The order of distinct mode products does not matter, in the sense that for
i\neq j
,

\mathcal{F}\times_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}\times_{j}\mathbf{A}_{j}=\mathcal{F}\times_{j}\mathbf{A}_{j}\times_{i}\mathbf{A}_{i}.

Finally, if
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{F}\times_{1}\mathbf{A}_{1}\times_{2}\cdots\times_{K}\mathbf{A}_{K}
, then we have the formula

\mathbf{C}_{(k)}=\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{F}_{(k)}\mathbf{A}_{\text{-}k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}},

where
\otimes
is the Kronecker product,
\mathbf{A}_{\text{-}k}:=\mathbf{A}_{K}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{A}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{k-1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1}
, and
\mathbf{C}_{(k)}
is the mode-
k
unfolding of
\mathcal{C}
. Hence, denote by
\text{vec}(\mathcal{X})
the vectorization of
\mathcal{X}
, which is to stack all model-1 fibres of
\mathcal{X}
into a column vector, we have

\text{vec}(\mathbf{C}_{(k)})=(\mathbf{A}_{\text{-}k}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{k})\text{vec}(\mathbf{F}_{(k)}).

In particular,

\text{vec}(\mathcal{C})=\text{vec}(\mathbf{C}_{(1)})=(\mathbf{A}_{\text{-}1}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1})\text{vec}(\mathbf{F}_{(1)})=(\mathbf{A}_{K}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{A}_{1})\text{vec}(\mathcal{F}).

8
Appendix: Proof

We first present three important lemmas under our model assumptions.

Lemma 1
.

Under Assumption (E1), (E2), (L1), (L2), (R1), we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right),

(8.1)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right).

(8.2)

In addition, if Assumption (R2) is satisfied, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)\geq C\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)\right)=1,

(8.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)\geq C\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)\right)=1,

(8.4)

for some
c\in(0,1]
and
C>0
.

In Lemma
1
, (
8.1
) provides an upper bound for the largest eigenvalue of
\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}
(and
\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}
), which facilitates the proof of Theorem
1
. (
8.3
) suggests that at least
\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor
largest eigenvalues of
\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}
(and
\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}
) are of the same order, which guarantees the validity of using eigenvalue ratio to detect the existence of factors, which will be further discussed in Remark
10
.

Lemma 2
.

Under Assumption (E1), (E2), (F1), (L1), (L2), (R1), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\rightarrow\Sigma_{F,k}

for some positive definite matrix
\Sigma_{F,k}
, with all eigenvalues bounded away from 0 and infinity. For
j\in[r_{k}]
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\asymp 1,

(8.5)

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\right)\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}},

(8.6)

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}},

(8.7)

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\asymp 1,

(8.8)

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}.

(8.9)

and for
j\in[\min(z_{k},r_{k})]
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}.

(8.10)

In addition, if Assumption (R2) is satisfied, then for
\min(z_{k},r_{k})+1\leq j\leq\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor-r_{k}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)\asymp\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right).

(8.11)

The weak serial dependence of factors and errors are quantified in the following lemma.

Lemma 3
.

Define
\mathbf{A}_{f,T}
and
\mathbf{A}_{e,T}
similar to
\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T}
in Assumption (R2). Define
\mathbf{A}_{f,T^{2}}
to be the
T^{2}\times T^{2}
fourth moment matrix of the MA process
f_{t,l,j}^{(k)}
for any
k,l,j
, and define
\mathbf{A}_{e,T^{2}}
and
\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T^{2}}
similarly. Then, under Assumption (E1), (E2) and (F1), we have the following results: For
\mathbf{A}_{T}
can either be
\mathbf{A}_{f,T}
,
\mathbf{A}_{e,T}
or
\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T}
,

\displaystyle{}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}=O(T),\ \ \|\mathbf{A}_{T}\|_{F}^{2}=O(T).

(8.12)

For
\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}
can either be
\mathbf{A}_{f,T^{2}}
,
\mathbf{A}_{e,T^{2}}
or
\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T^{2}}
,

\displaystyle{}\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}=O(T^{2}),\ \ \|\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}\|_{F}^{2}=O(T^{2}).

(8.13)

Moreover,

\displaystyle{}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{e,T})}=O(T),\ \ \text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T})}=O(T),

(8.14)

\displaystyle{}\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{e,T}\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}=O(T^{2}),\ \ \mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T}\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}=O(T^{2}),

(8.15)

\displaystyle{}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{f,T})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{e,T^{2}})}=O(T^{2}),\ \ \text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{f,T})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T^{2}})}=O(T^{2}),

(8.16)

\displaystyle{}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T^{2}})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{e,T^{2}})}=O(T^{2}),\ \ \text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T^{2}})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T^{2}})}=O(T^{2}).

(8.17)

Remark 10
.

Note that Lemma
2
implies the following result for eigenvalue ratio:
For
j\leq r_{k}-1
,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{j+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}\asymp\frac{d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}}{d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j+1}}};

For
r_{k}+1\leq j\leq\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor-r_{k}-1
,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{j+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}\asymp 1;

For
j=r_{k}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{j+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}\asymp\frac{d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,r_{k}}}}{\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)}\rightarrow\infty.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{r_{k}+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}\asymp\frac{d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,1}}}{\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)}\rightarrow\infty.

(8.18)

(
8.18
) implies that at least one of ratio of subsequent eigenvalues
\left\{\frac{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{j+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)},j\in[r_{k}]\right\}
goes to infinity. This is essential to detect the existence of factors. Note that in the special case when all factors have the same strength, we should have
\frac{\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{r_{k}+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}\rightarrow\infty
, which recovers the result in
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
.

In contrast, when there is no factor exist, we should have that

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{j+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}=\frac{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{j+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)}\asymp 1

for
j=1,...,\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor
by (
8.3
) in Lemma
1
. Thus, every ratio (actually until
\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor
) of subsequent eigenvalues remains bounded. In this way, we can use eigenvalue ratio to detect the existence of factors accordingly: when there is factor exist, at least one of the eigenvalue ratio will goes to infinity; when there is no factor, all eigenvalue ratios (until
\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor
) remains bounded. Note that this is different from estimating the number of factors, since we can only know that there is factor exist, but we do not really know how many factors and what the factor strengths are. However, in the special case when all factors are of the same strength, we have
\frac{\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{r_{k}+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}\rightarrow\infty
and
\frac{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}{\lambda_{j+1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}\asymp 1
for
j\neq r_{k}
, which is how
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
estimates the number of factors. In the same spirit, the eye-ball test of
Lam and Yao, 2012
also uses eigenvalue ratios in a similar manner to deduce the number of factors in a statistical factor model.

Proof of Lemma
1
.

By Assumption (E1), (E2), (R1), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{t,k}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k)}\mathbf{e}_{t}^{(k)}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{d_{\text{-}k}}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon,\ell}^{(k)})^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{t,\ell}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{t,k,1}+\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{t,k,2}

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{t,k,1}:=\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k)}\mathbf{e}_{t}^{(k)}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{t,k,2}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{d_{\text{-}k}}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon,\ell}^{(k)})^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{t,\ell}^{(k)}
. Using such decomposition, the error matrix can be written as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}=\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}+\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2},

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}=\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{1,k,1},...,\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{T,k,1}\right)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}=\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{1,k,2},...,\widetilde{\mathbf{e}}_{T,k,2}\right)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
. Then we can deal with
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}
separately using random matrix theory. We first look at
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}
. Similar to assumptions in
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
, we further write the matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}=L_{e,k,1}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{e,k,1}R_{e,k,1}^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
U_{e,k,1}\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{k}}
are iid random variables with uniformly bounded fourth moment by Assumption (R1), and
L_{e,k,1}\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times T}
and
R_{e,k,1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}\times d_{k}}
are time-serial and cross-sectional covariance matrices. By Assumption (E1),
R_{e,k,1}=\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k)}\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k){\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}
, and
L_{e,k,1}=\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}
. Next, we need to bound the spectral norm of
L_{e,k,1}
and
R_{e,k,1}
. Assumption (E1) implies
\big\|R_{e,k,1}\big\|=O(d_{\text{-}k})
. For
\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}
, since it is symmetric,
\left\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}\right\|_{1}=\left\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}\right\|_{\infty}
, and

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\max_{t}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left|(\mathbf{A}_{e,T})_{ts}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{v=0}^{T}\left|\sum_{q\geq 0}a_{e,q}a_{e,q+v}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\sum_{q\geq 0}|a_{e,q}|\right)^{2}\leq C

(8.19)

by Assumption (E2). Thus,
\big\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}\big\|\leq\sqrt{\left\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}\right\|_{1}\left\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}\right\|_{\infty}}=\left\|\mathbf{A}_{\mathbf{e},T}\right\|_{1}\leq C
.
Bai and Yin, 1993
and
Latala, 2005
show that
\lambda_{1}\left(U_{e,k,1}U_{e,k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}/T\right)\rightarrow(1+\sqrt{d_{k}/T})^{2}
. Thus, similar to the result obtained by
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
(see also
Moon and Weidner, 2015
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}}{T}\right)\leq\big\|L_{e,k,1}\big\|\big\|R_{e,k,1}\big\|\big\|U_{e,k,1}U_{e,k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}/T\big\|=O_{p}\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right).

For
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}
, similarly we can write it as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}=L_{e,k,2}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{e,k,2}R_{e,k,2}^{\frac{1}{2}}

where
R_{e,k,2}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon}^{(k)}
, and
L_{e,k,2}=\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T}
. By Assumption (E2),
\|R_{e,k,2}\|=O(d_{\text{-}k})
, and we have
\big\|L_{e,k,2}\big\|\leq C
by applying the same analysis as above. Accordingly,

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}\right)\leq\big\|L_{e,k,2}\big\|\big\|R_{e,k,2}\big\|\big\|U_{e,k,2}U_{e,k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}/T\big\|=O_{p}\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}}{T}\bigg\|+\bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}\bigg\|+\bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}}{T}\bigg\|+\bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}\bigg\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)+2\bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq O_{p}\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)+\sqrt{\bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}}{T}\bigg\|\bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}}\bigg\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right),

which implies (
8.1
), and

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{\sqrt{T}}\bigg\|^{2}\bigg\|\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right),

which implies (
8.2
)

To show (
8.3
), we focus on
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}
. Note that Assumption (R2) is parallel to Assumption (D) in
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
. Following Lemma A.7. in
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{\max{(T,d_{k})}}\right)\geq Cd_{\text{-}k}\right)=1

for some
c\in(0,1]
and
C>0
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}\right)\geq C\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)\right)=1

for some
c\in(0,1]
and
C>0
. Finally, by Weyl’s inequalities,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}\right)}-\sqrt{\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}}{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\asymp\sqrt{d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)},

which implies (
8.3
), since
\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}}{T}\right)=o_{p}\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)
under Assumption (R2). Similarly, following the same argument as Lemma A.7. in
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}\right)\geq C\left(d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)\right)=1

for some
c\in(0,1]
and
C>0
, so

\displaystyle\sqrt{\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\lambda_{\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,2}}{T}\right)}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{k,1}}{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\asymp\sqrt{d_{\text{-}k}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)},

which implies (
8.4
). This completes the proof of Lemma
1
.
\square

Proof of Lemma
2
.

We consider the case
K=3
without loss of generality. By Assumption (F1), we know each element of
\mathcal{F}_{t}
are independent with mean 0 and variance 1. Suppose we want to estimate
\mathbf{A}_{1}
. For
i,j\in[r_{1}]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\widetilde{f}_{t,1,i}\widetilde{f}_{t,1,j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{q=1}^{r_{3}}\sum_{u=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{v=1}^{r_{3}}colSum(\mathbf{A}_{2})_{p}colSum(\mathbf{A}_{3})_{q}colSum(\mathbf{A}_{2})_{u}colSum(\mathbf{A}_{3})_{v}\mathbb{E}(f_{t,ipq}f_{t,juv})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{q=1}^{r_{3}}colSum(\mathbf{A}_{2})_{p}^{2}colSum(\mathbf{A}_{3})_{q}^{2}

\displaystyle\asymp s_{2}s_{3}=s_{\text{-}1}

when
i=j
, and 0 otherwise. Thus,
\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{f}_{t,1,i}\ddot{f}_{t,1,j}\right)\asymp 1
for
i=j
, and 0 otherwise, which implies that
\Sigma_{F,k}
is a diagonal matrix with all diagonal elements bounded away from 0 and infinity.

Next, we show (
8.5
) – (
8.7
). To apply random matrix theory, similar to
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
, we can further write the matrix
\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}
as

\displaystyle\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}=L_{f,k}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{f,k}R_{f,k}^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
U_{f,k}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times T}
are iid random variables with uniformly bounded fourth moment by Assumption (R1), and
L_{f,k}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times r_{k}}
and
R_{f,k}\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times T}
are cross-sectional and time-serial covariance matrices. By Assumption (F1),
L_{f,k}=I_{r_{k}}
, and
R_{f,k}=\mathbf{A}_{f,T}
. Following similar analysis as (
8
), we have
\|R_{f,k}\|_{1}\leq C
. Thus,
\big\|R_{f,k}\big\|\leq\sqrt{\left\|R_{f,k}\right\|_{1}\left\|R_{f,k}\right\|_{\infty}}=\left\|R_{f,k}\right\|_{1}\leq C
.
Since we assume
r_{k}=o(T)
,
Bai and Yin, 1993
and
Latala, 2005
show that
\lambda_{1}\left(U_{f,k}U_{f,k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}/T\right)-(1+\sqrt{r_{k}/T})^{2}\rightarrow 0
and
\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(U_{f,k}U_{f,k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}/T\right)-(1-\sqrt{r_{k}/T})^{2}\rightarrow 0
. Similar to the result obtained by
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
(see also
Moon and Weidner, 2015
), since the largest eigenvalues of
R_{f,k}
and
L_{f,k}
are bounded, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}/T\right)\leq\big\|L_{f,k}\big\|\big\|R_{f,k}\big\|\big\|U_{f,k}U_{f,k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}/T\big\|=O_{p}(1).

Similarly, since
\lambda_{1}(R_{f,k})=\big\|R_{f,k}\big\|\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}/T\right)=\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(U_{f,k}R_{f,k}U_{f,k}^{T}/T\right)=\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(U_{f,k}^{T}U_{f,k}R_{f,k}/T\right)\geq\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(U_{f,k}^{T}U_{f,k}/T\right)\lambda_{1}(R_{f,k})\geq 1

as
r_{k},T\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, we have
\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\asymp 1
for
j\in[r_{k}]
.

Next, for
j\in[r_{k}]
, we know

\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)=\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}}{T}\right)

and

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\right)\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}}{T}\right)\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\right)\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right).

Since
\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\asymp 1
for
j\in[r_{k}]
, it follows that
\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}}{T}\right)\asymp\lambda_{j}(\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k})
for
j\in[r_{k}]
. Similarly,

\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\right)=\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)=\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{D}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

and

\lambda_{j}(\mathbf{D}_{k})\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{D}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\leq\lambda_{j}(\mathbf{D}_{k})\lambda_{1}\left(\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

By Assumption (L1),
\lambda_{j}(\mathbf{D}_{k})\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}
and
\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\asymp 1
for
j\in[r_{k}]
(this also holds when elements of
\mathbf{A}_{k}
are random and independent). Thus, for
j\in[r_{k}]
, we have
\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\asymp\lambda_{j}(\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k})\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}
.

Next, we will show (
8.8
) – (
8.9
) accordingly. To start with, note that

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\bigg\|+\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\bigg\|+\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}}{\sqrt{T}}\bigg\|^{2}\bigg\|\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\bigg\|=2\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\bigg\|\asymp 1.

And

\displaystyle\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}-\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)-\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T^{2}}\right).

(8.20)

We next obtain an upper bound for
\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T^{2}}\right)
. Note that
\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times 1}
is a random vector, with its
i
-th element to be
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{f}_{t,i}
. For its moment bounds,
\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{f}_{t,i}\right)^{2}=\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\mathbf{1}_{T}=O(T)
and
\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{f}_{t,i}\right)^{4}=\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{f,T^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}=O(T^{2})
by Lemma
3
. Therefore, following similar argument in previous analysis, we can decompose
\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{T}=\sqrt{T}\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}U
, where
U\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{k}\times 1}
has iid random entries with bounded second and fourth moments. Thus,

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T^{2}}\right)=\frac{1}{T^{2}}\lambda_{1}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{T^{2}}O_{p}(r_{k}T)=O_{p}\left(\frac{r_{k}}{T}\right)=o_{p}(1),

which implies
\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\succeq 1
by (
8
). Therefore,
\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)
for
j\in[r_{k}]
.

Next, for
j\in[r_{k}]
, it is easy to see

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\right)\lambda_{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}\right)\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right),

which implies
\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}
for
j\in[r_{k}]
.

Observe that
\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)^{2}=\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
, and
\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)=\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)
. Hence to show (
8.10
), following the similar argument as Theorem 1 in
Freyaldenhoven, 2022
, for
j\in[r_{k}]
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)}-\sqrt{\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\succeq\sqrt{d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}}-\sqrt{O_{p}\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)}\asymp\sqrt{d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}}.

(8.21)

where the second line follows from Lemma
1
and the last line follows from Assumption (L2). Similarly, for
j\in[r_{k}]
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)+\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ +2\sqrt{\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)}\sqrt{\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}+O_{p}\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right)\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}.

(8.22)

Therefore,
\lambda_{j}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}\right)\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,j}}
for
j\in[r_{k}]
.

Finally, to show (
8.11
), we apply similar technique as Lemma A.8 in
Ahn and Horenstein, 2013
. For simplicity of expression, in the following proof, we write
\mathbf{M}_{T}=\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
and
\mathbf{M}=(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{M}_{T})^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{M}_{T}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}})^{-1}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\mathbf{M}_{T}
. Then
rank(\mathbf{M})\leq r_{k}
, and

\displaystyle\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}_{T}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}=\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}\right)\mathbf{M}_{T}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}\right)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{T}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}.

Hence, for
r_{k}+1\leq j\leq\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor-r_{k}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{T}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}_{T}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{j-r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{T}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle+\lambda_{r_{k}+1}\left[\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}\right)\mathbf{M}_{T}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}\right)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{j-r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{T}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right),

since
\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}\right)\mathbf{M}_{T}\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}\right)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
is positive semi-definite and has rank at most
r_{k}
. Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\lambda_{j-r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{T}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{j-r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{T}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{j-r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}_{T}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right),

\displaystyle\lambda_{j+r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}_{T}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{T}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)+\lambda_{r_{k}+1}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{j}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{M}_{T}-\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}_{T}\mathbf{M}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right).

Therefore, for
r_{k}+1\leq j\leq\lfloor c\min{(T,d_{k})}\rfloor-r_{k}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{j+r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}_{T}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)\leq\lambda_{j}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}_{T}\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}\right)\leq\lambda_{j-r_{k}}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{M}_{T}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right),

which implies (
8.11
) with the result of Lemma
1
. This completes the proof of Lemma
2
.
\square

Proof of Lemma
3
.
For
\mathbf{A}_{T}
can either be
\mathbf{A}_{f,T}
,
\mathbf{A}_{e,T}
or
\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T}
, we have already shown that
\|\mathbf{A}_{T}\|_{1}=O(1)
in the Proof of Lemma
1
and Lemma
2
(see (
8
) for example). Hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\leq\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|(\mathbf{A}_{T})_{ts}|\leq T\|\mathbf{A}_{T}\|_{1}=O(T).

In addition, it is not difficult to see every entry of
\mathbf{A}_{T}
has absolute value bounded above by 1, hence,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{A}_{T}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|(\mathbf{A}_{T})_{ts}|^{2}\leq\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|(\mathbf{A}_{T})_{ts}|\leq T\|\mathbf{A}_{T}\|_{1}=O(T).

Next, for
\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}
can either be
\mathbf{A}_{f,T^{2}}
,
\mathbf{A}_{e,T^{2}}
or
\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\max_{t_{1},s_{1}}\sum_{t_{2},s_{2}=1}^{T}\left|(\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}})_{t_{1}s_{1},t_{2}s_{2}}\right|\leq 2\max_{q_{1}q_{2}}\sum_{q_{3},q_{4}}\left|a_{q_{1}}a_{q_{2}}a_{q_{3}}a_{q_{4}}\right|\leq 2\left(\sum_{q\geq 0}|a_{q}|\right)^{4}\leq C.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{T}\leq\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|(\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}})_{ts}|\leq T^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}\|_{1}=O(T^{2}).

Similarly, we can also observe that every entry of
\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}
has absolute value bounded above by 1. To see this, take
f_{t,l,j}^{(k)}
for example, note that for the MA process
f_{t}:=f_{t,l,j}^{(k)}
for any
(l,j,k)
,
\left|\mathbb{E}(f_{t_{1}}f_{t_{2}}f_{t_{3}}f_{t_{4}})\right|\leq 0.5\left[\mathbb{E}(f_{t_{1}}^{2}f_{t_{2}}^{2})+\mathbb{E}(f_{t_{3}}^{2}f_{t_{4}}^{2})\right]\leq 0.25\left[\mathbb{E}(f_{t_{1}}^{4})+\mathbb{E}(f_{t_{2}}^{4})+\mathbb{E}(f_{t_{3}}^{4})+\mathbb{E}(f_{t_{4}}^{4})\right]\leq 1
, because
\mathbb{E}(f_{t_{i}}^{4})=\sum_{q\geq 0}a_{q}^{4}\leq\left(\sum_{q\geq 0}a_{q}^{2}\right)^{2}=1
for any
t_{i}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|(\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}})_{ts}|^{2}\leq\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}|(\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}})_{ts}|\leq T^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{T^{2}}\|_{1}=O(T^{2}).

(
8.14
) – (
8.17
) can be easily implied by (
8.12
) and (
8.13
). To see this, first note that for any matrices
A
and
B
,
\|A\otimes B\|_{F}=\|A\|_{F}\|B\|_{F}
, and
|\text{vec}{(A)}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(B)}|\leq\|A\|_{F}\|B\|_{F}
by Cauchy-Schwarz, which implies (
8.14
), (
8.16
) and (
8.17
). Finally,
\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{e,T}\mathbf{1}_{T^{2}}\leq T^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{e,T}\|_{1}=T^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\|_{1}\|\mathbf{A}_{e,T}\|_{1}=O(T^{2})
, which gives (
8.15
). This completes the proof of Lemma
3
.
\square

Proof of Theorem
1
. Note that we have

\displaystyle{}\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{X}}_{k}}{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\left(\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}+\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{A}_{k}\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+R_{k},

(8.23)

where

\displaystyle{}R_{k}=\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}+\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}+\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}.

(8.24)

We estimate
\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{k,(z_{k})}
as the first
z_{k}
eigenvectors of (
8
). Let’s define
\mathbf{U}_{k,(z_{k})}
to be the matrix consisting of the first
z_{k}
columns of
\mathbf{U}_{k}
, and
\mathbf{B}
be its orthogonal complement. Then
\mathbf{U}_{k,(z_{k})}
is an invariant subspace for
\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}
and

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{U}_{k,(z_{k})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\\
\mathbf{B}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\end{array}\right]\mathbf{A}_{k}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left[\mathbf{U}_{k,(z_{k})}\ \ \mathbf{B}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{G}_{k,(z_{k})}&0\\
0&\Lambda_{k,(z_{k})}\end{array}\right],

where
\mathbf{G}_{k,(z_{k})}
is a
z_{k}\times z_{k}
diagonal matrix consisting the largest
z_{k}
eigenvalues of
\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}
, and
\Lambda_{k,(z_{k})}
is a
(d_{k}-z_{k})\times(d_{k}-z_{k})
diagonal matrix where the first
r_{k}-z_{k}
entries are the
z_{k}+1
to
r_{k}
eigenvalues of
\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}
, and the remaining entries are all 0’s. In this way, we can apply Lemma 3 of
Lam et al., 2011
and know that there exists
\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{k,(z_{k})}
(with
\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{k,(z_{k})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{k,(z_{k})}=\mathbf{I}_{z_{k}}
) such that
\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{k,(z_{k})}=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{k,(z_{k})}\mathbf{P}_{k,(z_{k})}
where
\mathbf{P}_{k,(z_{k})}
is orthogonal matrix, so that

\displaystyle{}\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{k,(z_{k})}-\mathbf{U}_{k,(z_{k})}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\bigg\|\mathbf{A}_{k}\left[\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}\right]\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}+R_{k}\bigg\|}{\text{sep}(\mathbf{G}_{k,z_{k}},\Lambda_{k,z_{k}})}

\displaystyle\preceq\frac{\bigg\|\mathbf{A}_{k}\left[\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}\right]\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\bigg\|+\big\|R_{k}\big\|}{d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,z_{k}}}}.

(8.25)

Next, we bound the norms on the numerator of (
8
). For the first term,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathbf{A}_{k}\left[\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}\right]\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathbf{A}_{k}\big\|^{2}\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\asymp d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,1}}\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}\bigg\|.

By Assumption (F1),
\{f_{t,l,j}^{(k)}\}
is a linear process with absolutely summable autocovariance sequence. In Lemma
2
, we have shown that elements of
\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}
retain the covariance structure of
\{f_{t,l,j}^{(k)}\}
, so each row of
\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}
is a linear process with absolutely summable autocovariance sequence, which satisfies Assumption (R2) in
Lam, 2021
. Thus, applying the result from Lemma 3 (or more specifically, equation (8.26)) of
Lam, 2021
implies

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}\bigg\|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{T}}\right),

(8.26)

which further gives

\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathbf{A}_{k}\left[\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}\right]\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\bigg\|=O_{p}\left(d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,1}}\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{T}}\right).

Next, we bound the norm of
R_{k}
. First, note that
\big\|\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\|\leq\big\|\mathbf{I}_{T}\big\|+\big\|\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\big\|=2
. Bounding the squared norm of each term on the right hand side of (
8.24
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\bigg\|^{2}\bigg\|\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}}{T^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg\|^{2}\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\right)\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,1}}\right)O_{p}\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right),

(8.27)

where the last line follows from Lemma
1
and Lemma
2
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\mathbf{A}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}{T}\bigg\|^{2}=O_{p}\left(d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,1}}\right)O_{p}\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}}{T}\right)\right).

As for the last term,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\lambda_{1}\left(\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\right)^{2}=O_{p}\left(\frac{d_{\text{-}k}^{2}}{s_{\text{-}k}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{d_{k}^{2}}{T^{2}}\right)\right)

(8.28)

by Lemma
1
and Lemma
2
.

For the first two terms on the right hand side of (
8.24
), there may exist potentially better bounds. To see this, note that we can equivalently write

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\mathbf{A}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\left(\mathbf{I}_{T}-\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\right)\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathbf{A}_{k}\big\|^{2}\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\bigg\|^{2}+\big\|\mathbf{A}_{k}\big\|^{2}\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(d_{k}^{\alpha_{k,1}}\right)\left(\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\bigg\|^{2}+\bigg\|\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}\bigg\|^{2}\right).

Since
\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}
and
\frac{\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\frac{1}{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}\mathbf{1}_{T}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}}{T}
are
r_{k}\times d_{k}
matrices with each element having a certain rate of convergence, we actually can have a better rate by simply counting the numbers of elements of them. More specifically, the
(i,j)
element of
\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}
is

\displaystyle\left(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)_{ij}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{f}_{t,k,i}\ddot{e}_{t,k,j},

where
\ddot{f}_{t,k,i}
is the
i
-th entry of
\ddot{\mathbf{f}}_{t,k}
, and
\ddot{e}_{t,k,j}
are defined similarly. We now focus on bounding the Frobenius norm of
\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}
. Note that
\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{(i,j)\in(r_{k},d_{k})}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)_{ij}^{2}
and

\displaystyle{}\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\sqrt{Var(\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{2})}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}+O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{4}\right)-\left(\mathbb{E}\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}\right)^{2}}\right).

(8.29)

Thus, we need to obtain bounds for
\mathbb{E}\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{4}
. We start with
\mathbb{E}\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\|_{F}^{2}
. For each entry of
\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{f}_{t,k,i}\ddot{e}_{t,k,j}\right)^{2}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{f}_{t,k,i}\ddot{f}_{s,k,i}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{e}_{t,k,i}\ddot{e}_{s,k,i}\right).

By Assumption (F1),

\displaystyle{}\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{f}_{t,k,i}\ddot{f}_{s,k,i}\right)=\sum_{q\geq 0}a_{f,q}a_{f,q-|t-s|}=\left(\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\right)_{ts}.

(8.30)

Similarly, by Assumption (E1) and (E2),

\displaystyle{}\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{e}_{t,k,j}\ddot{e}_{s,k,j}\right)=\frac{1}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k)}\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k){\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}\right)_{jj}\left(\mathbf{A}_{e,T}\right)_{ts}+\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon}^{(k)}\right)_{jj}\left(\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T}\right)_{ts}\right].

(8.31)

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{s_{\text{-}k}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{A}_{f,T}\right)_{ts}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k)}\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k){\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}\right)_{jj}\left(\mathbf{A}_{e,T}\right)_{ts}+\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon}^{(k)}\right)_{jj}\left(\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T}\right)_{ts}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k)}\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k){\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}\right)_{jj}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{e,T})}+\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon}^{(k)}\right)_{jj}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T})}\right],

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right)_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{r_{k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\left[\text{tr}(\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k)}\boldsymbol{\Psi}^{(k){\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}})\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{e,T})}+\text{tr}(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\epsilon}^{(k)})\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{f,T})}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}\text{vec}{(\mathbf{A}_{\epsilon,T})}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(r_{k}d_{k}T\frac{d_{\text{-}k}}{s_{\text{-}k}}\right),

(8.32)

where the last line follows from Assumption (E1) and Lemma
3
. Next, for
\mathbb{E}\left\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right\|_{F}^{4}
, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k}\right\|_{F}^{4}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{r_{k}}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{d_{k}}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{r_{k}}\sum_{j_{2}=1}^{d_{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k})_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{2}(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k})_{i_{2}j_{2}}^{2}\right],

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k})_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{2}(\ddot{\mathbf{F}}_{k}\ddot{\mathbf{E}}_{k})_{i_{2}j_{2}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{f}_{t,k,i_{1}}\ddot{e}_{t,k,j_{1}}\right)^{2}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ddot{f}_{t,k,i_{2}}\ddot{e}_{t,k,j_{2}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t_{1}=1}^{T}\sum_{s_{1}=1}^{T}\sum_{t_{2}=1}^{T}\sum_{s_{2}=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left(\ddot{f}_{t_{1},k,i_{1}}\ddot{f}_{s_{1},k,i_{1}}\ddot{f}_{t_{2},k,i_{2}}\ddot{f}_{s_{2},k,i_{2}}\right)
